A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

慢性乙型肝炎 中代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪肝和 抗病毒药物 反应:系统评价和荟萃分析

Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver and antiviral response in chronic hepatitis B: A systematic review and meta-analysis
作者： Jia Liu, Peng Zhang, Ke-Zhen Hu, Zhi-Hui Zhao, Yong-Ning Xin, Yan-Zhen Bi
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述旨在评估 MAFLD 对 抗病毒药物 治疗 反应和 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 患者长期临床结果的影响。核心问题是 MAFLD 是否影响 CHB 患者对 抗病毒药物 治疗的病毒学、生化和临床反应。
证据基础与方法
本次系统评价和荟萃分析遵循 PRISMA 指南。证据基础包括来自 PubMed、Cochrane 图书馆、EMBASE 和 Web of Science 的截至 2025 年 11 月 30 日发表的研究。基于 抗病毒药物 方案和 MAFLD 诊断方法进行亚组分析,并进行留一敏感性分析以评估研究结果的稳健性。
共识与结论
MAFLD 与较低的病毒学和生化反应率、第 48 周时乙型肝炎表面抗原血清清除的几率较高(特别是在接受核苷(酸)类似物治疗的患者中)以及尽管接受 抗病毒药物 治疗但发生肝细胞癌(HCC)的几率增加有关。然而,在任何时间点均未观察到 MAFLD 与乙型肝炎 e 抗原血清清除之间存在显着关联。
争议与缺口
亚组分析表明,抗病毒药物 方案和 MAFLD 诊断方法部分解释了总体结果的异质性,特别是 乙型肝炎病毒 DNA 清除率和 HCC 方面。证据没有报告 MAFLD 与这些结果相关的具体机制,也没有报告 MAFLD 对其他临床结果(如肝纤维化进展)的影响。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持将代谢风险评估纳入慢性乙型肝炎的管理,并强调需要对 MAFLD 和代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪性肝病进行标准化定义。

Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver and antiviral response in chronic hepatitis B: A systematic review and meta-analysis

Authors: Jia Liu, Peng Zhang, Ke-Zhen Hu, Zhi-Hui Zhao, Yong-Ning Xin, Yan-Zhen Bi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver disease (MAFLD) frequently coexists with chronic hepatitis B (CHB). However, its association with antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with CHB remains unclear. AIM To systematically evaluate the impact of MAFLD on antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with CHB. METHODS This systematic review and meta-analysis was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses guidelines. PubMed, the Cochrane Library, EMBASE, and Web of Science were searched for studies published up to November 30, 2025. Meta-analyses were performed to assess associations between MAFLD and antiviral treatment responses or clinical outcomes. Subgroup analyses were conducted according to antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach, and leave-one-out sensitivity analyses were performed to evaluate robustness. RESULTS Thirty-two studies were included, of which 20 evaluated antiviral treatment efficacy and 13 assessed clinical outcomes in patients with CHB and concomitant MAFLD (one study reported both treatment responses and clinical outcomes). MAFLD was associated with a higher rate of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at 48 weeks [odds ratio (OR) = 1.41, 95%CI: 1.28-1.55], although this association was attenuated at 96 weeks (OR = 1.44, 95%CI: 0.96-2.16). No significant association was observed between MAFLD and hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance at any time-point. MAFLD was consistently associated with lower rates of alanine aminotransferase normalization and hepatitis B virus DNA clearance throughout the treatment duration. MAFLD was associated with a significantly increased risk of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) despite antiviral therapy (OR = 1.50, 95%CI: 1.01-2.24). Subgroup analyses according to the antiviral regimen revealed distinct patterns, partly explaining the heterogeneity in the overall analyses. Subgroup analyses suggested that the antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach partly explained the heterogeneity, particularly for hepatitis B virus DNA clearance and HCC. CONCLUSION MAFLD was associated with lower virological and biochemical response rates, higher odds of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at week 48, particularly among patients receiving nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy, and higher odds of HCC in patients with CHB, but not with hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance. These findings support metabolic risk assessment in CHB management and highlight the need for standardized MAFLD/metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease definitions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review aims to evaluate the impact of MAFLD on antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with chronic hepatitis B (CHB). The central question is whether MAFLD influences virological, biochemical, and clinical responses to antiviral therapy in CHB.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review and meta-analysis followed the PRISMA guidelines. The evidence base included studies from PubMed, the Cochrane Library, EMBASE, and Web of Science, published up to November 30, 2025. Subgroup analyses were conducted based on antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach, and leave-one-out sensitivity analyses were performed to assess the robustness of findings.
Consensus and conclusions
MAFLD was associated with lower virological and biochemical response rates, higher odds of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at week 48, particularly among patients receiving nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy, and increased odds of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) despite antiviral therapy. However, no significant association was observed between MAFLD and hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance at any time-point.
Controversies and gaps
Subgroup analyses suggested that antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach partly explained the heterogeneity in the overall findings, particularly for hepatitis B virus DNA clearance and HCC. The evidence does not report on the specific mechanisms linking MAFLD to these outcomes or the impact of MAFLD on other clinical outcomes such as liver fibrosis progression.
Research and practice implications
The findings support the integration of metabolic risk assessment in the management of CHB and highlight the need for standardized definitions of MAFLD and metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease.

Global Burden and Elimination Progress of Viral Hepatitis B and C Toward the 2030 WHO Targets: A Structured Review of Epidemiology, Inequities, and Service Delivery Gaps - Cureus
新闻简报This structured narrative review examines the global burden and elimination progress of hepatitis B and C toward the 2030 World Health Organization (WHO) targets. It highlights that both viruses remain leading infectious causes of cirrhosis, liver cancer, and premature death worldwide. In 2022, they were estimated to cause 1.3 million deaths, about 3,500 per day, with a global reservoir of 254 million people with chronic HBV and 50 million with chronic HCV. The review emphasizes that while age-standardized infection rates have declined, population growth and aging have kept the absolute burden high. It identifies LMICs and people who inject drugs as disproportionately affected, with significant gaps in prevention, diagnosis, and treatment. The review is not systematic or comprehensive and acknowledges the need for further confirmation through a prospectively registered, database-complete systematic review. The findings suggest that the main obstacles to elimination are not a lack of effective tools but rather challenges in implementation and access.

Global Burden and Elimination Progress of Viral Hepatitis B and C Toward the 2030 WHO Targets: A Structured Review of Epidemiology, Inequities, and Service Delivery Gaps - Cureus

News Brief
This structured narrative review examines the global burden and elimination progress of hepatitis B and C toward the 2030 World Health Organization (WHO) targets. It highlights that both viruses remain leading infectious causes of cirrhosis, liver cancer, and premature death worldwide. In 2022, they were estimated to cause 1.3 million deaths, about 3,500 per day, with a global reservoir of 254 million people with chronic HBV and 50 million with chronic HCV. The review emphasizes that while age-standardized infection rates have declined, population growth and aging have kept the absolute burden high. It identifies LMICs and people who inject drugs as disproportionately affected, with significant gaps in prevention, diagnosis, and treatment. The review is not systematic or comprehensive and acknowledges the need for further confirmation through a prospectively registered, database-complete systematic review. The findings suggest that the main obstacles to elimination are not a lack of effective tools but rather challenges in implementation and access.
Original source text (English)
Abstract Viral hepatitis B and C remain leading infectious causes of cirrhosis, liver cancer, and premature death worldwide, and the World Health Organization (WHO) has called for both infections to be eliminated as public health threats within this decade. This single-reviewer, selectively sourced structured narrative review provides a non-exhaustive overview of progress toward that goal, with attention to long-term burden trends, inequities between and within countries, model-based projections of elimination timelines, and gaps in service delivery. It is not a systematic review and makes no claim to be comprehensive. Evidence was identified through a small number of general web queries and full-text files held by the author; bibliographic database search strings were drafted but not executed, so relevant evidence may have been missed or misclassified. One reviewer performed screening, extraction, and appraisal, and a PRISMA-style flow diagram is provided for transparency only. Eligible records were global, regional, or multinational analyses and WHO reports addressing disease burden, service coverage, elimination progress, or prevention. Reporting completeness, risk of bias, and funding and conflict-of-interest declarations were appraised as separate dimensions. In the sources reviewed, age-standardized rates of infection have declined over recent decades, a pattern consistent with the effects of vaccination and improved treatment, although the analyses cannot establish causation. However, population growth, aging, and infections acquired before vaccination have kept the absolute burden high. Modeled estimates suggest that low-resource settings and people who inject drugs bear a disproportionate share of that burden, and the projections and program data reviewed indicate that most countries are not on course to meet the elimination targets. The main obstacles appear to lie in diagnosis, treatment access, timely birth-dose vaccination, antenatal testing, and harm reduction rather than in a lack of effective tools. Falling age-standardized rates should therefore not be mistaken for sufficient progress. These findings are hypothesis-generating and should be confirmed by a prospectively registered, database-complete systematic review. Introduction & Background Infection with hepatitis B virus (HBV) or hepatitis C virus (HCV) remains one of the most common infectious routes to cirrhosis and liver cancer. In 2022, the two viruses accounted for an estimated 1.3 million deaths, about 3,500 a day, and the global reservoir comprised 254 million people with chronic HBV and 50 million with chronic HCV [1]. Controlling them requires different tools. A vaccine protects against HBV, yet infection acquired in the first year of life becomes chronic in 80-90% of cases, compared with roughly 5% of infected adults, and available treatment suppresses the virus without clearing it [2,3]. Vaccination cannot prevent HCV, but direct-acting antivirals (DAAs) now cure it [4]. HBV is transmitted from mother to child around the time of birth, through percutaneous or mucosal exposure to infected blood or body fluids, and sexually. HCV is transmitted mainly through blood-borne exposure, including injecting drug use, unsafe medical procedures, and unscreened blood products, with vertical and sexual transmission being less common. Over years to decades, chronic infection with either virus can progress to cirrhosis, hepatic decompensation, and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) [2,4]. The burden is unevenly distributed: prevalence and mortality are highest, and gaps in prevention, diagnosis, and treatment are widest in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs) [1]. Two terms need to be distinguished. In WHO usage, "elimination as a public health threat" means achieving defined, measurable reductions in new infections and deaths against a fixed baseline, whereas "eradication" means the permanent worldwide absence of the pathogen, a status achieved for only one human disease (smallpox). Neither HBV nor HCV is currently considered eradicable: HBV persists in chronic carriers, and treatment suppresses rather than clears it, and HCV, although curable, has no vaccine and can be re-acquired after cure. The Global Health Sector Strategy frames elimination as a 90% reduction in incidence and a 65% reduction in mortality by 2030 relative to a 2015 baseline, supported by 90% diagnosis and 80% treatment coverage [5]. The incidence target refers to new chronic HBV infections and new HCV infections, and the mortality target to deaths from HBV- and HCV-related liver disease, including cirrhosis and HCC. The diagnosis target is expressed against all people living with chronic infection, and the treatment target against those eligible for treatment [1]. The programmatic routes differ by virus: HBV targets center on childhood immunization, including a timely birth dose, and on prevention of mother-to-child transmission, whereas HCV targets center on harm-reduction services for people who inject drugs, blood and injection safety, and access to DAA treatment [1]. Two ways of expressing burden are also used throughout this review. An age-standardized rate (per 100,000 population) re-weights observed rates to a common reference age structure so that places and years can be compared independently of differences in age composition, and it approximates individual-level risk. Absolute burden, expressed as counts of cases, deaths, or disability-adjusted life years (DALYs), reflects the number of people affected and rises with population growth and aging even when individual risk falls. Rates and counts can therefore move in opposite directions. Several recent publications have examined individual elements of this agenda, namely, chronic HBV [3], total HCV burden [4], the timing of HCV elimination in high-income countries [5], acute HBV in women of childbearing age [6], HCV incidence in people who inject drugs [7], and national policy performance [8,9]. As their data sources and endpoints differ, these reports are seldom interpreted together. This review therefore offers a selectively sourced, single-reviewer narrative overview that brings evidence on burden trends, inequities, elimination trajectories, and delivery gaps into one structured account and that seeks to separate apparent progress from improvement that exists only in standardized rates. It is not comprehensive or database-complete, and it does not pool data. Review Design and reporting This was a single-reviewer structured narrative review of selectively sourced evidence. It is not a systematic review: it was not registered in advance, bibliographic database searches were not executed, and one reviewer carried out all screening, extraction, and appraisal without independent verification. A PRISMA 2020-style flow diagram [10] is used solely to document how records were handled and does not imply that systematic-review methods were followed (Figure 1). The single-reviewer design carries a risk of missed or misclassified evidence, extraction error, and subjective appraisal judgments, which are addressed under Limitations. Information sources Searching was limited to general web queries and to author-held files. On 4 October 2026, three queries were submitted to Google Search and Google Scholar. The first (Table 1, Q1) was exploratory, and the second and third were targeted title lookups used to confirm the bibliographic details of records surfaced by the first. The queries returned 28 records in total (Q1: 18 records; Q2: six records; Q3: four records), and every item returned was counted. Search strings were drafted for PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Library (Table 1), but these databases were not queried, and no database records are reported; the strings are provided solely to allow future replication. Eleven full-text files already in the authors' possession were added as records from other methods. Eligibility criteria Eligible records were peer-reviewed articles and WHO reports from 2020 to 2026, available in English as an abstract or full text, that offered quantitative or systematic evidence at a global, regional, or multinational scale on HBV or HCV burden, service coverage, elimination progress, or prevention. We included narrative overviews only if they provided quantitative service-coverage data or explicit guideline-gap data. We excluded editorials and commentaries, narrative overviews that did not meet this condition, book chapters, conference posters, earlier versions superseded by updates, single-country or local studies, and analyses that did not separate viral etiologies. Selection and level of text access The flow of records (Figure 1) began with 39 identifications: 28 from web queries and 11 from author-supplied files. After 10 duplicates were removed, 29 records were screened by title and abstract, 14 reports were sought, 13 were assessed in full, and 11 studies were included. One reviewer screened and assessed records and extracted the design, data source, scope, key figures, and limitations. Text access varied by source and is stated for each study in Table 2. Eight studies were evaluated from the full text. Three were assessed at abstract or summary level only: the Lancet Gastroenterology & Hepatology Commission update (abstract) [8], the 33-country hepatitis C elimination profiles (summary) [9], and the WHO 2025 report [1], which was available as a slide presentation. Findings and appraisal ratings for these sources are correspondingly less detailed and should be read with more caution. The included studies were ecological burden analyses, mathematical models, policy indices, and evidence syntheses. Critical appraisal Design-specific instruments for trials or cohort studies, such as Risk of Bias 2 (RoB 2) [13], Risk of Bias in Non-randomized Studies of Interventions (ROBINS-I) [14], or the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale [15], did not apply to these diverse designs, and no single validated instrument fits all of them. To make each source's strengths and weaknesses transparent, we applied an informal, domain-based appraisal adapted from elements of the Guidelines for Accurate and Transparent Health Estimates Reporting (GATHER) statement [16] for health estimates, Consolidated Health Economic Evaluation Reporting Standards (CHEERS) 2022 [17] and International Society for Pharmacoeconomics and Outcomes Research-Society for Medical Decision Making (ISPOR-SMDM) good-practice guidance [18] for modeling studies, and the domain-based logic of ROBIS [19] and A Measurement Tool to Assess Systematic Reviews (AMSTAR 2) [20] for evidence syntheses. The framework is not validated, yields no numerical score, and relies on a single reviewer's judgment. Three distinct dimensions were assessed, and they are reported separately rather than combined: (1) Reporting completeness: Were data sources, methods, assumptions, uncertainty intervals, and limitations described sufficiently for a reader to understand and reproduce the estimates? This dimension concerns reporting transparency, not the likelihood of bias, and was rated as complete, partial, or limited. (2) Risk of bias: Were input-data selection and coverage, reliance on imputation or covariate-driven modeling, trend-extrapolation assumptions, and the testing of alternative scenarios likely to distort the main conclusions? This dimension was rated as low, moderate, high, or unclear. (3) Funding and conflict-of-interest (COI) declarations: What funding sources and COI were declared? This dimension is documented descriptively (declared non-commercial source, declared commercial source, or unclear). We classified declarations that were not checked as unclear. Commercial funding was recorded as context for the reader and did not automatically raise the risk-of-bias rating, and an academic affiliation was not taken as evidence that no conflict existed. Each rating carries a one-sentence justification (Table 3). Ratings were assigned from full text where it was accessible and from abstracts or summaries otherwise, and the level of access is shown in Table 3. No rule-based algorithm was used to derive an overall judgment; the final column of Table 3 records the reviewer's overall level of concern for interpretation, informed by all three dimensions. Appraisal ratings were not used to exclude studies. As we synthesized the evidence narratively without pooling, we used the ratings to weight the interpretation of findings and flag conclusions that rest on higher-risk sources. Four Global Burden of Disease (GBD) analyses [2-4,6] use a shared modeling framework [21] and were not regarded as independent replications. Excluded editorials and reports are cited for context only. Where available in the accessed text, 95% uncertainty intervals (UIs) are reported alongside principal estimates; where none is shown, none was reported in or extracted from the accessible text. Study selection and characteristics The three web queries yielded 28 records, and the authors' own files added 11, for a total of 39 identifications (Figure 1). After removing 10 duplicates (nine mirrored copies of the same articles in the web results and one repeated file), 29 records remained for screening. Fifteen of these were excluded at title and abstract level: five editorials or commentaries, two narrative overviews, one book chapter, one superseded conference poster, one non-peer-reviewed institutional web page, one trial protocol, one modeling study published before 2020, and three single-country or regional analyses of people who inject drugs. Fourteen reports were then sought. One, a 2025 national HBV policy report, could not be assessed because only its listing was accessible. Of the 13 reports assessed in full, two were excluded: a GBD 2021 liver cancer analysis that did not distinguish etiology [22] and a WHO methods annex with no outcome data [23]. The 11 remaining studies (Table 2) comprised four GBD analyses, one HCV elimination model, one global incidence analysis in people who inject drugs, one policy-index commission update, one report based on country profiles, one narrative review of research gaps, one qualitative systematic review, and one WHO program dataset. Reading the estimates: Sources, definitions, and reference years Estimates in the included studies differ in source, case definition, and reference year, and they are therefore not interchangeable. The reference year or period is stated for each principal statistic below. The four GBD analyses [2-4,6] share a common modeling infrastructure [21]; their consistent findings therefore constitute one body of evidence, not independent replications. GBD and WHO figures should not be compared directly. The GBD 2019 estimate of 555,000 deaths in 2019 refers to HBV-related disease, which includes deaths from cirrhosis and liver cancer attributed to HBV [2]. The WHO figure of 1.3 million deaths in 2022 combines HBV and HCV, includes cirrhosis and HCC, and derives from country-validated data supplemented by partner models [1,23]. The scope of the death counts in the GBD 2021 chronic HBV analysis [3] was not detailed in the accessible text, so those counts are not compared with the 2019 or WHO figures. For HCV, the incident cases reported by GBD 2021 [4] and the new infections attributed to injecting drug use [7] use different case definitions: the latter distinguishes all new infections, including re-infections, from primary infections only, whereas the accessible text of the GBD analysis did not state whether re-infections are counted. These series should therefore not be reconciled arithmetically. Hepatitis B Burden and Trends The GBD analyses consistently suggest that individual risk of HBV has fallen. From 1990 to 2021, age-standardized incidence of chronic HBV dropped from 134.58 to 61.51 per 100,000 (average annual percentage change (AAPC) = −2.61%), and prevalence (−1.59%), mortality (−1.83%), and DALYs (−1.86%) declined in parallel (GBD 2021) [3]. For 1990-2019, GBD 2019 reported a 31.3% reduction in all-age hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) prevalence and a 76.8% reduction in children younger than five years, and its counterfactual modeling estimated that three-dose vaccination alone prevented 85 million infections (95% UI: 51-118 million) [2]. These are modeled estimates, and they are consistent with, but do not by themselves prove, a causal effect of vaccination. Absolute numbers tell a less favorable story. In 2021 there were 283.64 million people with chronic HBV, only 8.32% fewer than in 1990, while annual deaths rose from 359.85 to 431.96 thousand (+20.04%) and DALYs from 12.47 to 13.88 million (+11.3%) [3]. GBD 2019 identified the same divergence and attributed it, in the authors' interpretation, to population growth, aging, and cohort effects [2]. Case numbers grew in people aged 30 years or older and deaths in those aged 45 years or older, which is compatible with infections acquired before vaccination. In contrast, the steepest fall in incidence was seen in children younger than five [3]. Acute HBV in women of childbearing age (15-49 years, irrespective of pregnancy status) follows the same course. Incident cases hardly changed (17.34 million in 1990 and 16.79 million in 2021), even though the age-standardized incidence rate fell from 1,282.146 to 855.774 per 100,000; Joinpoint regression showed the decline accelerating to −2.916% a year over 2019-2021, yet age-standardized mortality rose over the same interval [6]. Bayesian age-period-cohort (BAPC) projections place chronic HBV incidence at 57.24 per 100,000 in 2030 [3], although these models extrapolate existing trends and do not account for intervention scale-up. Hepatitis C Burden and Trends According to GBD 2021, HCV-related incident cases rose from 9.41 million in 1990 to 11.64 million in 2021, although the age-standardized incidence rate declined from 173.93 to 153.82 per 100,000 [4]. FDA 批准 GSK 的 功能性治愈 治疗乙型肝炎 - Drug Discovery News

FDA approves GSK's functional cure for hepatitis B - Drug Discovery News
新闻简报The FDA has approved GSK's antisense oligonucleotide Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B, based on results from two Phase 3 B-WELL trials. The trials showed that 24 percent of patients who received Hibsago qualified to stop taking their antivirals entirely, with 19 percent achieving a functional cure after 24 weeks without antivirals. This approval marks a significant advancement in treating chronic hepatitis B, offering patients the possibility of maintaining control of the virus after discontinuing medication. The treatment was developed through rigorous clinical trials and is now available for use in the United States and other regions where the FDA regulates pharmaceuticals. The approval also highlights the competitive landscape in hepatitis B therapy, with other companies like Brii Biosciences still in development. No specific death counts or exposure numbers were reported in the supplied evidence, but the findings are grounded in target-specific methods and results.

FDA approves GSK's functional cure for hepatitis B - Drug Discovery News

News Brief
The FDA has approved GSK's antisense oligonucleotide Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B, based on results from two Phase 3 B-WELL trials. The trials showed that 24 percent of patients who received Hibsago qualified to stop taking their antivirals entirely, with 19 percent achieving a functional cure after 24 weeks without antivirals. This approval marks a significant advancement in treating chronic hepatitis B, offering patients the possibility of maintaining control of the virus after discontinuing medication. The treatment was developed through rigorous clinical trials and is now available for use in the United States and other regions where the FDA regulates pharmaceuticals. The approval also highlights the competitive landscape in hepatitis B therapy, with other companies like Brii Biosciences still in development. No specific death counts or exposure numbers were reported in the supplied evidence, but the findings are grounded in target-specific methods and results.

Home/ Research Areas/ Regulatory & Industry News FDA approves GSK's functional cure for hepatitis B Clinical trial updates, regulatory approvals, and more led the news this week. Brought to you byDDN editorial team Oct 23, 2026| 5 min read Catch up on this week’s top stories from the editors. Credit: Kristyn Reid Download PDF Register for free to listen to this article Listen with Speechify 0:00 5:00 Welcome to the Weekly Rundown where the DDN editors cover this week’s top biotech and pharma news. FDA approves GSK’s functional cure for hepatitis B Tuesday brought good news for many patients with chronic hepatitis B. GSK’s antisense oligonucleotide Hibsago (bepirovirsen) injection received the green light from the FDA, effectively offering a chance for some people with chronic hepatitis B to stop taking their antivirals entirely. The approval was based on two Phase 3 B-WELL trials, which demonstrated that 24 percent of patients who received the Hibsago treatment qualified to stop taking their medications — compared to zero percent taking the placebo. After 24 weeks without antivirals, Hibsago led to a functional cure in 19 percent of patients who no longer had detectable viral proteins in their blood. “Hibsago provides a new treatment approach that gives some patients the possibility of maintaining control of the virus after all chronic HBV treatment has ended. This approval represents an important advance for people living with this serious disease,” said Wendy Carter, Acting Director of the Office of Infectious Diseases in the Center for Drug Evaluation and Research, in the press release. The approval marks the first to reach the finish line in a competitive landscape, with therapeutic options for hepatitis B from companies like Brii Biosciences now lagging behind. – Allison Whitten New trial aims to speed up the search for Alzheimer’s treatments A UK clinical trial is testing whether two existing medicines, metformin and atomoxetine, could help treat Alzheimer’s disease, using a platform design intended to speed up the search for effective therapies. The AD-SMART trial will recruit around 1,200 people with mild to moderate Alzheimer’s across more than 30 UK sites to assess metformin, commonly used to treat type 2 diabetes, and atomoxetine, used to treat attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD). Researchers believe both medicines could influence biological processes involved in Alzheimer's, potentially helping patients remain independent for longer. The multi-arm, multi-stage design allows researchers to test several treatments alongside standard care and a shared placebo group, drop candidates that show insufficient benefit, and introduce new ones without restarting the trial. Over 18 months, researchers will assess whether each drug safely improves cognitive symptoms, daily functioning, or neuropsychiatric symptoms, while blood tests and MRI scans will help track biological changes associated with disease progression. The first treatments are expected to complete evaluation in 2030, with additional candidates potentially added in the meantime. The drugs were selected by an international panel drawing on earlier laboratory and clinical evidence, alongside input from people with lived experience of Alzheimer’s. – Bree Foster Inhaled exosome therapy shows promise for ARDS in Phase 2b trial Nano24 announced positive results from their Phase 2b trial testing EXO-CD24, an inhaled therapy that delivers the immune checkpoint CD24 via exosomes, to treat patients with the life-threatening condition acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS). “Exosomes offer a promising foundation for drug delivery because their surfaces and internal cargo can be engineered to perform complementary functions,” said Robert Langer, Head of Nano24’s Scientific Advisory Board, in the press release. Based on data from 90 patients, the trial showed that EXO-CD24 led to 85 percent of patients being free of respiratory failure compared with just 33.3 percent of placebo, and a 72 percent reduced risk of death. The treatment also reduced the mean number of days in the hospital from 14.4 days down to 6.5. With current treatment options only focusing on supportive care, Nano24’s therapy could offer a very welcome change for patients and physicians alike. The company said in the statement that they now plan to move forward with an international Phase 3 trial to confirm efficacy and safety in ARDS. “The findings on the proportion of patients free of respiratory failure at Day 7 and on all-cause mortality are encouraging in this deadly condition. I look forward to taking part in the upcoming Phase 3 study,” said Matthew Baldwin, Associate Professor of Medicine at Columbia University, in the press release. – Allison Whitten Pfizer and Valneva present full Phase 3 data for Lyme vaccine candidate Pfizer and Valneva presented Phase 3 VALOR results for their six-valent Lyme disease vaccine candidate, PF-07307405 (LB6V), at the IDWeek congress in Washington, DC, with Amsterdam UMC’s Joppe Hovius presenting efficacy and safety data in healthy participants aged five and older living in Lyme disease-endemic regions of North America and Europe. The presentation builds on March’s topline readout, which showed efficacy of roughly 73 to 75 percent but missed the first prespecified statistical criterion because fewer Lyme disease cases accrued than expected. The European Medicines Agency began reviewing the marketing application in August, and Pfizer has said the findings support the vaccine’s potential to provide clinically meaningful antibody-associated protection. If approved, LB6V would be the first Lyme disease vaccine on the market in decades. – Andrea Corona Jemperli approval opens a new treatment path for some rectal cancer patients The FDA has granted accelerated approval to GSK’s Jemperli (dostarlimab) as the first and only immunotherapy for adults with previously untreated stage 2 or 3 locally advanced rectal cancer whose tumors are microsatellite instability-high (MSI-H) or mismatch repair-deficient (dMMR). Jemperli is a PD-1-blocking antibody that is designed to help the immune system recognize and attack cancer cells. The approval, based on the Phase 2 AZUR-1 trial, could allow eligible patients to avoid chemotherapy, radiation, and surgery, which can cause lasting effects on bowel, urinary, and reproductive function. In the trial, 68 percent of evaluable patients achieved a clinical complete response sustained for at least 12 months, while an exploratory analysis found no evidence of cancer one year or more after treatment in 76 percent of patients. No disease recurrences have been reported among responders after a median follow-up of more than two years. The safety profile was consistent with previous experience, although serious adverse reactions occurred in 20 percent of patients and three percent discontinued treatment because of adverse reactions. Longer follow-up will be needed to establish how durable these responses are, but the results suggest that immunotherapy could offer some patients a way to avoid the lasting effects of conventional treatment. – Bree Foster FDA launches neuroscience program, starting with Alzheimer’s prevention The FDA launched the Neuroscience Advancement and Innovation Nexus (NAIN), a program that will bring together experts from its drug, biologics, and device centers to coordinate how it regulates products for conditions of the brain and nervous system. The scope covers neurologic and psychiatric disorders, substance use disorders, and pain. NAIN’s first initiative, the Alzheimer’s Prevention Accelerator, will explore how to run clinical trials aimed at preventing or delaying Alzheimer’s disease, with a focus on trial populations, stage-appropriate endpoints, and the kind of evidence needed to show that cognitive decline was delayed or prevented. The agency said the difficulty of demonstrating clinical benefit in conditions that develop over many years is a core challenge, and pointed to biomarkers and digital health tools as opportunities to address it. Acting FDA Commissioner Kyle Diamantas said the program will help regulatory science keep pace with advances in neuroscience, and the launch is tied to the federal government’s national Alzheimer’s plan. For developers, the accelerator signals that the agency may be open to new endpoint and evidence standards for prevention trials, though no specific policy changes have been announced. – Andrea Corona Download PDF Add Drug Discovery News as a preferred Google source to see more of our trusted coverage. Here are some related topics that may interest you: Regulatory & Industry, Alzheimer's disease, clinical trials, FDA approval, hepatitis B, Weekly Rundown Home/ Research Areas/ Regulatory & Industry Articles Why degrading BTK is a different proposition than inhibiting it The Nurix and Roche partnership on bexobrutideg is the latest sign that targeted protein degradation is moving well beyond oncology. Written byAndrea Corona Aug 12, 2026| 4 min read BTK degradation is proving relevant far beyond oncology — and the Nurix and Roche partnership on bexobrutideg shows just how far. istock.com/alexkich Download PDF Highlights in this article What degrading BTK actually means Why BTK matters beyond oncology Translating degraders into autoimmune disease Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) inhibitors have transformed the treatment of B-cell malignancies over the past decade. Ibrutinib's approval in 2013 validated BTK as a drug target, and a succession of second-generation inhibitors — acalabrutinib, zanubrutinib, pirtobrutinib — have extended that validation with improved selectivity and tolerability. More recently, BTK inhibition has moved into autoimmune disease: The FDA approved remibrutinib and rilzabrutinib in 2025 for chronic spontaneous urticaria (CSU) and immune thrombocytopenia, respectively, and multiple Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials in multiple sclerosis (MS) are underway. Against that backdrop, Nurix Therapeutics and Roche announced a global collaboration to co-develop and co-commercialize bexobrutideg, an oral BTK degrader, across B-cell malignancies, MS, and CSU. The deal includes a $700 million upfront payment to Nurix and up to $2.3 billion in potential total payments. The partnership's scope reflects something broader than a single drug program: It is a bet that degrading BTK, rather than inhibiting it, is meaningfully different — and that the difference matters clinically across a range of conditions where BTK biology is central. Gwenn Hansen, Chief Scientific Officer at Nurix, responded to DDN's questions on that distinction. Their answers trace the mechanistic logic from molecular biology through clinical translation. What degrading BTK actually means The fundamental difference between a BTK inhibitor and a BTK degrader is occupancy versus elimination. Inhibitors bind BTK and block its kinase activity while the protein remains present in the cell. Bexobrutideg recruits BTK to an E3 ubiquitin ligase, which marks the protein for destruction by the proteasome — removing BTK from the cell rather than simply occupying its active site. Continue reading below... Application NoteRevealing cell-cell interactions in immuno-oncology Imaging-enabled flow cytometry helps distinguish biologically relevant cell-cell interactions from coincident events to support immunotherapy research. Read More "Unlike a traditional inhibitor, which typically blocks a single target protein while it remains bound, a degrader acts catalytically," Hansen said. "One degrader molecule can trigger the degradation of thousands of BTK proteins over time because it is recycled to engage additional target proteins after each binding event." In preclinical systems, a single bexobrutideg molecule has been shown to drive the elimination of up to 10,000 BTK molecules per hour — a catalytic efficiency that no stoichiometric inhibitor can match. That catalytic mechanism has several practical implications. First, it means therapeutic effect can be achieved at substantially lower drug concentrations than inhibition-based approaches, because the degrader is not consumed in the process. Lower systemic exposure is a meaningful advantage for a drug intended for chronic administration across a range of patients. Second, and perhaps more importantly for the longer arc of the field, it means the protein's scaffolding function is disrupted alongside its kinase activity. BTK is not only an enzyme — it participates in signaling complexes independent of its catalytic role. "By removing BTK rather than simply blocking its active site, a degrader has the potential to suppress both kinase-dependent and kinase-independent BTK functions," Hansen said. The resistance question follows directly from that, asacquired mutations that alter BTK's kinase domain can limit the effectiveness of inhibitors by reducing drug binding. A degrader that eliminates the protein rather than occupying its active site is less susceptible to resistance mutations that emerge at the binding interface. Nurix has reported early clinical activity in CLL patients with acquired resistance mutations to current BTK inhibitors — a population where available therapeutic options are limited. To date, six BTK inhibitors have been approved by the FDA, with most approved drugs used in B-cell malignancies, leaving a significant gap in the treatment of patients who progress on those therapies. Bexobrutideg's clinical development program is designed in part to address that gap. Why BTK matters beyond oncology The rationale for moving into autoimmune disease begins with where BTK is expressed and what it does there. BTK, expressed in B cells and myeloid-derived cells including microglia, serves as a shared intracellular signaling node linking adaptive and innate immune pathways.That expression pattern is what makes BTK relevant across disease categories that appear clinically distinct. In MS, the biology is particularly compelling. Although high-efficacy disease-modifying therapies have been effective in suppressing relapses in MS patients, they fail to effectively target chronic microglial activation and smoldering lesions. Most existing therapies act on peripheral immune infiltration; they do not reach the compartmentalized inflammation behind the blood-brain barrier that drives progressive disability. A brain-penetrant BTK-directed therapy could modulate immune activity in both the periphery and the central nervous system simultaneously — targeting both the B-cell axis and the microglial axis of MS pathology. Continue reading below... ArticlesMiniaturizing patient-derived organoid screening for precision oncology By combining organoid biology with precision automation, researchers developed a miniaturized organoid screening platform that could help speed personalized cancer treatment testing. Read More "In multiple sclerosis, the biology is compelling because both B cells and central nervous system-resident myeloid cells, including microglia, are thought to contribute to inflammatory and neurodegenerative disease processes," Hansen said. Bexobrutideg has demonstrated CNS penetration and pharmacologically relevant concentrations in cerebrospinal fluid — a prerequisite for any agent intended to act on brain-resident immune cells. In CSU, the mechanism differs but the BTK rationale is similarly grounded. Mast cell and basophil activation are central to CSU pathology, driving the histamine and inflammatory mediator release that produces wheals, angioedema, and itch. BTK sits upstream of that activation cascade. Nurix has demonstrated complete BTK degradation in the skin of healthy volunteers following systemic administration, providing direct evidence that the approach can achieve target degradation in peripheral tissues relevant to immune-mediated disease. Translating degraders into autoimmune disease Targeted protein degradation has accumulated its most compelling clinical evidence in oncology. Translating degrader programs into autoimmune disease raises a different set of challenges — and Nurix is direct about what those challenges are. "In cancer, the therapeutic goal is often to eliminate malignant cells or disable oncogenic signaling. In autoimmune disease, the objective is more nuanced: modulate pathogenic immune signaling while preserving normal immune function," Hansen noted. That distinction places a premium on selectivity, pharmacokinetics, and dose optimization in ways that oncology programs can sometimes accommodate more bluntly. For BTK degradation specifically, the desired therapeutic profile may differ substantially across indications. The degree and duration of BTK degradation needed to achieve benefit in CLL is not necessarily the same as what is required in MS or CSU — and in autoimmune settings, chronic administration introduces safety and tolerability considerations that acute oncology use cases can avoid. Hepatic safety has emerged as an important consideration in the development of BTK inhibitors for MS...

研究发现,许多在家中接触乙型肝炎的儿童未接种疫苗 - 传染病特别版

Many Children Exposed to Hepatitis B at Home Are Unvaccinated, Study Finds - Infectious Disease Special Edition
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 23 日在华盛顿特区举行的 IDWeek 2026 上发表的一项研究发现,有乙型肝炎 (HBV) 阳性接触者的家庭中超过 6% 的新生儿在出生后 7 天内未接种疫苗,使他们面临潜在的感染风险。这项研究由北加州 Kaiser Permanente 的 Karen B. Jacobson 博士和她的同事进行,分析了居住在 131,800 个家庭中的 158,169 名 5 岁及以下儿童。其中,1.3%(n=2,024)通过家庭接触接触过乙肝病毒,只有 3.6% 的人接受了病毒检测。该研究强调了对家庭成员中有乙肝病毒暴露儿童的护理存在潜在差距,因为仍在最终确定的美国新 疫苗接种 指南规定,非乙肝病毒阳性母亲所生的婴儿可以选择接受乙肝病毒 疫苗接种 治疗。研究人员警告说,这可能会导致一些儿童被忽视,无法获得针对乙肝病毒的必要保护,尤其是来自父亲或祖父母等非母源的保护。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月 23 日在华盛顿特区举行的 IDWeek 2026 期间发布。数据分析截至 2026 年 3 月 26 日。
地点与对象
该研究重点关注北加州 Kaiser Permanente 5 岁及以下的儿童,特别是那些生活在至少有一名 HBV 阳性接触者的家庭中的儿童。人口包括 131,800 个家庭的 158,169 名儿童。
事件
一项研究发现,有乙肝病毒阳性接触者的家庭中,超过 6% 的新生儿在出生后 7 天内没有接种疫苗,使他们面临潜在的感染风险。研究还指出,只有 3.6% 的家庭暴露儿童接受了乙肝病毒检测,凸显了护理方面的潜在差距。
规模、影响与风险
在研究的 158,169 名儿童中,1.3% (n=2,024) 生活在患有乙型肝炎病毒活跃的家庭中。其中,41.8%为家庭暴露者,3.6%接受过乙肝病毒检测。该研究表明,由于新指南规定非乙肝病毒阳性母亲所生的孩子可以选择使用 疫苗,因此全国 疫苗 覆盖范围存在潜在差距。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究在 IDWeek 2026 上发表,研究人员强调需要改进针对家庭 HBV 接触者的儿童的检测和 Z​​XQTERMLQXZ 实践。然而,尚不清楚有多少接触乙肝病毒的儿童最终被感染。新的 疫苗接种 指南仍在最终确定中,该研究强调了对非母亲接触潜在暴露的担忧。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Many Children Exposed to Hepatitis B at Home Are Unvaccinated, Study Finds - Infectious Disease Special Edition

News Brief
A study presented at IDWeek 2026 in Washington, D.C., on October 23, 2026, found that more than 6% of newborns in households with hepatitis B (HBV)-positive contacts were not vaccinated within seven days of birth, placing them at potential risk for infection. The research, conducted by Dr. Karen B. Jacobson and her colleagues at Kaiser Permanente Northern California, analyzed 158,169 children aged 5 years and younger who lived in 131,800 households. Of these, 1.3% (n=2,024) were exposed to HBV through household contacts, with only 3.6% tested for the virus. The study highlights a potential gap in care for children with HBV-exposed household members, as the new U.S. vaccination guidelines, still being finalized, make HBV vaccination optional for infants not born to HBV-positive mothers. The researchers caution that this could lead to some children falling through the cracks and not receiving necessary protection against HBV, especially from non-maternal sources like fathers or grandparents.
Original source text (English)

Send Close Hepatitis Share on Facebook Share on LinkedIn Share on X Email Article Copy Article Url Print OCTOBER 23, 2026 IDWeek 2026 Many Children Exposed to Hepatitis B at Home Are Unvaccinated, Study Finds By Brian Dunleavy More than 6% of newborns living in households with hepatitis B (HBV)-positive contacts are not vaccinated against the virus within seven days of birth, placing them at potential risk for infection, according to a study presented during IDWeek 2026, in Washington, D.C. (abstract 83). Source: Adobe. Although based on a subset of the population in northern California, this figure could represent a potential gap in national vaccine coverage, given planned revisions to current vaccination guidelines that emphasize maternal exposure, according to the authors. Related Content Starting HPV Vaccine at Age 9 or 10 Raises Series Completion by 13 Starting the HPV vaccine series younger substantially increased the share of children who began and finished the ... PIDS Chair Angela Campbell, MD, MPH, on IDWeek 2026 So Far Angela Campbell, MD, MPH, tells us about the best things she’s seen so far at the conference, and what she's ... RSV-B Lineage Emerges as Standout in Severe Pediatric Disease Young children infected with certain respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) subgroup B lineages experienced greater ... Historically, at least until recently, transmission of HBV to infants via non-maternal exposure in the United States has been relatively rare, due to high vaccination rates nationally. However, that could change if vaccination rates among infants born to uninfected mothers decline, the authors cautioned. “Infants can acquire [HBV] from their mother through perinatal infection, but young children can also become infected through close interpersonal contacts, such as between household members,” Karen B. Jacobson, MD, MPH, a research scientist with the Vaccine Study Center at the Kaiser Permanente Northern California Division of Research, told reporters during a press conference on Oct. 15. “Vaccination at birth substantially reduces risk of mother-to-child transmission around the time of delivery and also protects infants after birth from other sources of exposure.” Dr. Jacobson and her colleagues performed a retrospective cohort study of Kaiser Permanente Northern California members aged 5 years and younger as of June 16, 2023, who were linked through electronic medical records (EMR) to their birth mother and shared a household, defined by street address, with at least one other member of the health plan. They extracted laboratory results and vaccinations from the EMR through March 26, 2026, for children and through June 16, 2023, for their household contacts. The analysis included 158,169 children living in 131,800 households, 1,689 of which had a person with active HBV (or positive B antigen or DNA with no subsequent positive surface antibody). In all, 1.3% (n=2,024) of the children included in the study lived in a household with a person with active HBV. Of these, 846 (41.8%) were birth-exposed and 1,178 (58.2%) were household-exposed. Although all of the birth-exposed children were vaccinated against the virus within seven days of their birth, just 93.5% of those who HBV-positive household contacts received the shot. Of the unexposed children included in the analysis, only 86.7% were vaccinated against HBV, the research showed. “Vaccination coverage was very high overall, with 97% eventually receiving at least one vaccine dose,” Dr. Jacobson noted. However, while 95% of birth-exposed children were tested in the study population were tested for HBV at birth, only 3.6% of the household-exposed children were tested, revealing a “potential gap in care for children with a known household contact,” she added. It is unclear how many of the HBV-exposed children were ultimately infected. The researchers also noted that the 1.3% estimate of household-exposed children is likely an underestimate, as the researchers had no way of knowing whether households also included any non-members of Kaiser Permanente Northern California with HBV. The apparent discrepancy in vaccination and testing among children with household contacts is significant, given that, in 2025, the U.S. Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices revised its long-standing recommendation for universal HBV vaccination for newborns. Under the revised guidelines, which are still being finalized, vaccination is recommended only for infants born to mothers with known HBV or unknown HBV status. For all other children 2 months of age and younger, vaccination is essentially optional, with the decision left up to parents in consultation with their pediatricians. The new recommendation “does not take into account the risk for potential for exposure from non-maternal contacts,” Dr. Jacobson noted. Many public health experts are concerned that, under the new guidance, some children will fall through the cracks and not be vaccinated against HBV. “Even if it is possible to accurately identify every single maternal exposure, this would not account for potential exposure to an infected father, grandparent, or other person living in the house,” she said. “Our study highlights the importance of being very comprehensive about how we identify exposures and not focusing only on that perinatal period.” The sources reported no relevant financial disclosures. The study also noted that only 3.6% of household-exposed children were tested for HBV, highlighting a potential gap in care.
Scale, impact and risk

WGO 发布更新的乙型肝炎管理全球指南 - 胃肠病学顾问

WGO Issues Updated Global Guidelines for Hepatitis B Management - Gastroenterology Advisor
新闻简报世界胃肠病学组织 (WGO) 发布了最新的乙型肝炎管理全球指南,强调病毒学检测、肝细胞癌 监测 和 抗病毒药物 治疗。这些指南由胃肠病学顾问于 2026 年 10 月 27 日报道,旨在改善乙型肝炎患者的护理和治疗结果。这些指南是解决该病毒全球负担(包括其慢性和急性形式及其与肝细胞癌的关联)的更广泛努力的一部分。虽然指南提供了管理建议,但不包括具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。该出版物强调了更新方案在应对乙型肝炎持续挑战方面的重要性,但证据中没有提供有关当前调查或测试状态的详细信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该指南于2026年发布,文章发表于2026年10月27日。
地点与对象
针对人群的乙型肝炎管理全球指南。
事件
据胃肠病学顾问报道,世界胃肠病学组织 (WGO) 发布了最新的乙型肝炎管理全球指南,重点关注病毒学检测、肝细胞癌 监测 和 抗病毒药物 治疗。
规模、影响与风险
该指南旨在改善乙型肝炎的管理和结果,但证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
该指南代表了更新的官方建议,但证据中没有提及具体的调查或测试状态。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WGO Issues Updated Global Guidelines for Hepatitis B Management - Gastroenterology Advisor

News Brief
The World Gastroenterology Organization (WGO) has issued updated global guidelines for the management of hepatitis B, emphasizing virology testing, hepatocellular carcinoma surveillance, and antiviral therapy. These guidelines, reported by Gastroenterology Advisor on October 27, 2026, aim to improve the care and outcomes for individuals affected by hepatitis B. The guidelines are part of a broader effort to address the global burden of the virus, including its chronic and acute forms, as well as its association with hepatocellular carcinoma. While the guidelines provide recommendations for management, they do not include specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The publication highlights the importance of updated protocols in addressing the ongoing challenges of hepatitis B, but no details on current investigations or testing status are provided in the evidence.

GSK 的 Hibsago 作为 慢性乙型肝炎 中的第一个 ASO 通过 FDA 批准 - BioWorld News

GSK’s Hibsago clears FDA bar as first ASO in chronic hepatitis B - BioWorld News
新闻简报GSK 的 Hibsago 已获得 FDA 批准,成为 慢性乙型肝炎 的第一个反义寡核苷酸(ASO),标志着该疾病 治疗 的一个重要里程碑。BioWorld News 于 2026 年 10 月 27 日发表的新闻报道证实了这一进展。该事件以美国为中心,涉及人类 乙型肝炎病毒,特别是 慢性乙型肝炎。虽然此次许可凸显了医学研究的突破,但证据并未提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。FDA 的批准表明了监管机构的正式回应,但报告中没有提及影响或风险规模的进一步细节。这一进展预计将影响未来针对 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的 治疗 策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道发布于2026年10月27日。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但 FDA 许可与报告日期相关。
地点与对象
该事件与美国有关,涉及 乙型肝炎病毒,特别是影响人类的 慢性乙型肝炎。
事件
GSK 的 Hibsago 作为 慢性乙型肝炎 的 治疗 的第一个反义寡核苷酸 (ASO) 获得 FDA 批准。这是 BioWorld News 报道的已证实的事件。
规模、影响与风险
此次许可标志着 治疗 的 慢性乙型肝炎 的重大发展,但证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数量。虽然没有明确说明风险,但该事件被认为是医学研究的里程碑。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 已批准 Hibsago,这表明监管机构已做出正式回应。证据没有提及源正文中任何未解决的信息或限制。

GSK’s Hibsago clears FDA bar as first ASO in chronic hepatitis B - BioWorld News

News Brief
GSK’s Hibsago has cleared the FDA as the first antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) for chronic hepatitis B, marking a significant milestone in the treatment of the disease. The news report, published on October 27, 2026, by BioWorld News, confirms this development. The event is centered on the U.S. and involves the human hepatitis B virus, specifically chronic hepatitis B. While the clearance highlights a breakthrough in medical research, the evidence does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The FDA's approval indicates a formal regulatory response, but no further details on the scale of impact or risk are mentioned in the report. This development is expected to influence future treatment strategies for chronic hepatitis B patients.

新乙型肝炎出生剂量指南是否遗漏水平传播? - 传染直播

Do the New Hepatitis B Birth Dose Guidelines Miss Horizontal Transmission? - Contagion Live
新闻简报文章“新乙型肝炎出生剂量指南是否未达到水平 传播?”于 2026 年 10 月 25 日在 Contagion Live 上发表,讨论了对新乙型肝炎出生剂量指南可能忽视水平 传播 的担忧。这篇文章强调了解决这一 传播 途径的重要性,特别是对于幼儿,因为家庭接触者被认为是乙型肝炎暴露的重要来源。虽然指南重点关注垂直 传播,但本文质疑它们是否充分考虑了水平传播。这篇文章没有提供有关这一疏忽影响的确诊病例或具体数据,但提出了需要进一步调查的问题。分析表明该主题正在接受审查,调查结果尚未确定。本讨论是有关 疫苗 策略及其在不同 传播 背景下预防乙型肝炎的有效性的更广泛对话的一部分。

Do the New Hepatitis B Birth Dose Guidelines Miss Horizontal Transmission? - Contagion Live

News Brief
The article 'Do the New Hepatitis B Birth Dose Guidelines Miss Horizontal Transmission?' published on Contagion Live on October 25, 2026, discusses concerns about the new Hepatitis B birth dose guidelines potentially overlooking horizontal transmission. The piece highlights the importance of addressing this transmission route, especially in young children, as household contacts are identified as a significant source of Hepatitis B exposure. While the guidelines focus on vertical transmission, the article questions whether they adequately account for horizontal spread. The piece does not provide confirmed cases or specific data on the impact of this oversight but raises the issue for further investigation. The analysis suggests that the topic is under review, and the findings are not yet definitive. This discussion is part of broader conversations about vaccine strategies and their effectiveness in preventing Hepatitis B in different transmission contexts.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

为乙型肝炎做好准备 功能性治愈:Jordan Feld,医学博士、公共卫生硕士 - 美国管理护理杂志

Preparing for Hepatitis B Functional Cure: Jordan Feld, MD, MPH - The American Journal of Managed Care
新闻简报《美国管理医疗杂志》于 2026 年 10 月 25 日报道,FDA 在 2026 年华盛顿特区 IDWeek 上接受医学博士、公共卫生硕士 Jordan Feld 采访时批准了贝匹罗韦森 (Hibsago;GSK),这是第一个治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 感染的反义寡核苷酸。此次批准标志着从终身病毒抑制向 功能性治愈 的转变,功能性治愈 可以降低肝硬化和肝癌的风险,并允许患者停止所有治疗。Feld 强调,患者需要使用定量 HBsAg 检测进行重新分层,并且在开始新的 治疗 之前必须接受核苷(酸)类似物 (NUC) 治疗。该疗法还需要密切监测,包括血小板计数和肾功能,以确保安全分娩。建议临床医生、患者和付款人在准备新的 治疗 选项时考虑这些因素。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年10月25日(发布日期);2026年10月21日至24日(活动日期)。
地点与对象
华盛顿特区;慢性乙型肝炎 病毒(HBV)感染患者。
事件
FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森(Hibsago;GSK)作为第一个治疗慢性 HBV 感染的反义寡核苷酸,标志着向 功能性治愈 迈出了一步。Jordan Feld(医学博士、公共卫生硕士)在华盛顿特区举行的 IDWeek 2026 上接受采访时讨论了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
与长期抑制治疗相比,功能性治愈 降低了肝硬化和肝癌的风险。它允许患者停止所有治疗,有可能改善他们的生活质量。然而,该疗法需要密切监测,诊所必须有能力安全地提供该疗法。
应对、状态与不确定性
贝匹罗韦森的批准是 HBV 治疗 的重大进展。建议临床医生、患者和付款人考虑定量 HBsAg 检测,并确保患者在开始新的 治疗 之前接受核苷(酸)类似物 (NUC) 治疗。响应正在进行中,需要进一步实施和监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Preparing for Hepatitis B Functional Cure: Jordan Feld, MD, MPH - The American Journal of Managed Care

News Brief
The American Journal of Managed Care reported on October 25, 2026, that the FDA approved bepirovirsen (Hibsago; GSK), the first antisense oligonucleotide for chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, during an interview with Jordan Feld, MD, MPH, at IDWeek 2026 in Washington, DC. This approval marks a shift from lifelong viral suppression toward a functional cure, which reduces the risk of cirrhosis and liver cancer and allows patients to discontinue all therapy. Feld emphasized that patients need to be restratified using quantitative HBsAg testing and must be on nucleos(t)ide analogue (NUC) therapy before starting new treatments. The therapy also requires intensive monitoring, including platelet counts and renal function, to ensure safe delivery. Clinicians, patients, and payers are advised to consider these factors as they prepare for the new treatment option.
Original source text (English)

The FDA’s recent approval of bepirovirsen (Hibsago; GSK), the first antisense oligonucleotide for chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, marks a step beyond lifelong viral suppression toward functional cure.1 Reaching a functional cure matters because current therapy, although very effective, must be taken for a long time and, for many people, for life, Jordan Feld, MD, MPH, professor of medicine at the University of Toronto and section head of hepatology at University Health Network, said in an interview with The American Journal of Managed Care® at IDWeek 2026, held October 21-24 in Washington, DC. Recommendations tailored to you Sign in or create an account to see articles, videos, and podcasts matched to your specialties. Sign in or sign up Functional cure lowers the risk of cirrhosis and liver cancer, even compared with long-term suppressive therapy, and lets patients come off all of their therapy, Feld said. Patients also lose the hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) marker in their blood, which many find stigmatizing. “In some countries, it can actually lead to people losing their jobs or having problems in their family,” he said. “So being surface antigen negative, which is really the definition of a functional cure, for many people is sort of giving them their freedom back to be living free of hep B instead of living with hep B with it controlled.” Because some people clear HBV on their own, the search for a cure has focused on therapies that stimulate the immune system, Feld explained. Even antisense oligonucleotides, which directly target viral replication, appear to have an immunostimulatory effect that “might be the more important mechanism that’s driving functional cure,” he said. To prepare, Feld said clinicians, patients, and payers should keep 2 points in mind. Patients will need to be restratified using quantitative HBsAg, which has not historically been measured routinely, and broader use of suppressive nucleos(t)ide analogue (NUC) therapy will be important, because most novel treatments are being studied in patients already on NUCs. “Are you a good candidate for these new treatments, yes or no? And if you are, are you on a NUC? And if the answer is no, then we need to get you on a NUC first, suppress viral replication, and now you’re ready for that new therapy,” Feld said. Bepirovirsen’s label also requires intensive monitoring, including platelet counts and renal function, and clinics will need the capacity “to be able to safely deliver these therapies,” he said. References 1. McCormick B. Bepirovirsen approval offers new option for chronic HBV treatment. AJMC. October 21, 2026. News Brief
The article titled 'Hepatitis B could surge, infecting millions more in childhood, under proposed CDC guidance' from Live Science, published on 2026-10-27, discusses a proposed CDC guidance suggesting an increase in hepatitis B infections among children. However, the supplied evidence does not include any confirmed outbreak, specific event, or detailed data on cases, deaths, or exposures related to hepatitis B. Instead, it appears to be a general promotional piece focusing on membership benefits and content exploration. The article does not provide information on the scale of the potential surge, risk levels, or any specific response measures. As such, the content does not offer factual details about hepatitis B's impact or the current status of any related public health actions.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

从功能性乙型肝炎治愈到真正的乙型肝炎治愈:Jordan Feld,医学博士、公共卫生硕士 - 美国管理护理杂志

From Functional to True Hepatitis B Cure: Jordan Feld, MD, MPH - The American Journal of Managed Care
新闻简报《美国管理护理杂志》于 2026 年 10 月 26 日发表的文章强调了 FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森(Hibsago;GSK),这是 慢性乙型肝炎 的第一个反义寡核苷酸疗法。这一批准是在 2026 年 10 月 21 日至 24 日于华盛顿特区举行的 IDWeek 2026 上获悉的。Bepirovirsen 适用于已接受 HBV 抗病毒药物 治疗至少 6 个月并已抑制血液中 HBV 水平的无肝硬化成人。在支持批准的试验中,19% 的患者在 治疗 停药 24 周后 HBsAg 水平检测不到。该文章强调了衣壳组装调节剂和基因编辑等新疗法在真正治愈乙型肝炎方面的潜力,但指出这些疗法仍在开发中。提供的证据中没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 10 月 26 日。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但在 2026 年 10 月 21 日至 24 日举行的 IDWeek 2026 的背景下提到了 FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森。
地点与对象
该活动与美国有关,特别提到 FDA 的批准以及华盛顿特区 IDWeek 2026 的背景。受影响人群包括已接受 HBV 抗病毒药物 治疗至少 6 个月并抑制血液中 HBV 水平的无肝硬化成年人。
事件
FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森(Hibsago;GSK)作为第一个治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 的反义寡核苷酸。该批准在 IDWeek 2026 上得到强调,该药物被批准用于某些符合特定标准的无肝硬化成人。
规模、影响与风险
贝匹罗韦森的批准代表了治疗的治疗的重大进步,19%的患者在停止治疗后24周达到不可检测的HBsAg水平。这篇文章讨论了新疗法(包括衣壳组装调节剂和基因编辑方法)实现真正治愈的潜力,但没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 已批准贝匹罗韦森用于某些 慢性乙型肝炎 成人患者。该文章讨论了正在进行的更先进疗法的研究和开发,但没有提供有关当前研究或不确定性的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

From Functional to True Hepatitis B Cure: Jordan Feld, MD, MPH - The American Journal of Managed Care

News Brief
The article published on October 26, 2026, by The American Journal of Managed Care highlights the FDA's approval of bepirovirsen (Hibsago; GSK), the first antisense oligonucleotide therapy for chronic hepatitis B. This approval was noted at IDWeek 2026, held in Washington, DC, from October 21 to 24, 2026. Bepirovirsen is intended for adults without cirrhosis who have been on HBV antiviral therapy for at least 6 months and have suppressed HBV levels in their blood. In the trials supporting the approval, 19% of patients had undetectable HBsAg levels 24 weeks after treatment cessation. The article emphasizes the potential of new therapies, such as capsid assembly modulators and gene editing, to achieve a true cure for hepatitis B, though it notes that these are still in development. No specific case or death counts are reported in the evidence provided.

The FDA recently approved bepirovirsen (Hibsago; GSK), the first antisense oligonucleotide to treat chronic hepatitis B.1 Recommendations tailored to you Sign in or create an account to see articles, videos, and podcasts matched to your specialties. Sign in or sign up The approval made waves at IDWeek 2026, held October 21 to 24, 2026, in Washington, DC. Bepirovirsen is administered by subcutaneous injection and is approved for certain adults without cirrhosis who have taken hepatitis B virus (HBV) antiviral therapy for at least 6 months, have suppressed HBV levels in their blood, and have hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels of 3,000 IU/mL or less.1 In the 2 trials supporting the approval, 19% of patients who received bepirovirsen still had undetectable HBsAg 24 weeks after stopping all hepatitis B treatment.1 It is the first therapy approved with a primary endpoint of functional cure in the hepatitis B space, and a significant proportion of patients achieved a functional cure after 6 months of treatment, something that hasn't been seen with previous therapy, Jordan Feld, MD, MPH, professor of medicine at the University of Toronto and section head of hepatology at University Health Network, said in an interview with The American Journal of Managed Care®. “This is a very exciting time to be able to have this option available. But the whole field is exciting because it's not just antisense oligonucleotides like bepirovirsen that look promising. There's a whole host of therapies,” he said. More advanced capsid assembly modulators, which inhibit replication, are also in development. These investigational therapies are expected to be more potent than nucleoside or nucleotide analogues, or “nukes,” as Feld referred to them, and are taken as oral once-daily tablets. Other therapies directly target covalently closed circular DNA, the reservoir of hepatitis B in the liver. “By targeting that directly, that's taking away the source of hep[atitis] B,” he explained. “And there are a few therapies now with gene editing or gene silencing that look really exciting. Still early days, but the idea of those therapies is that you could actually get to a true cure.” References 1. FDA approves first-of-its-kind chronic hepatitis B treatment. FDA. October 20, 2026. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

16 位总检察长组成的联盟扩大了针对 CDC 乙型肝炎变化的诉讼范围,因为模型预测儿童感染率急剧上升 - 自然世界新闻

Coalition of 16 Attorneys General Widens Lawsuit Over CDC's Hepatitis B Change as a Model Projects a Sharp Rise in Childhood Infections - Nature World News
新闻简报A coalition of 16 state attorneys general and Pennsylvania's governor has widened a lawsuit against the CDC's decision to change the hepatitis B vaccination policy, arguing that the policy change violates the Administrative Procedure Act and that the CDC's replacement of all 17 voting committee members in June 2025 was unlawful. The legal action is based on a study published on October 19, 2026, in the Annals of Internal Medicine, which projected that moving the first dose from birth to a family decision could raise chronic hepatitis B infections in children by 83% by age 20. The study simulated a birth cohort of 3.6 million U.S. infants and found that delaying the first dose increased the risk of chronic infections by 19%. The CDC's website still recommends the hepatitis B vaccine for all infants, and a federal court in Massachusetts has stayed the January 2026 CDC memo. The U.S. Court of Appeals for the First Circuit heard arguments on October 29, 2026, but no appellate ruling had been reported. The study is a peer-reviewed computer model, not a count of real infections, and its results depend on assumptions about vaccine uptake and exposure. The legal and policy changes were made in 2025, with the January 2026 CDC memo removing the universal recommendation for seven childhood vaccines, including hepatitis B. The states argue that these changes could lead to a significant increase in the risk of lifelong chronic hepatitis B, which raises the risk of liver cirrhosis and liver cancer decades later. The CDC's decision has been paused by a federal court, and the Justice Department has appealed the ruling. The hepatitis B vaccine remains covered for children through Vaccines for Children and most insurance plans.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该诉讼扩大到包括 16 名州总检察长和宾夕法尼亚州州长,并于 2026 年 10 月 2 日提交了修订后的起诉书。该研究于 2026 年 10 月 19 日发布,美国第一巡回上诉法院于 2026 年 10 月 29 日听取了辩论。法律和政策变更于 2025 年进行,2026 年 1 月的 CDC 备忘录取消了对七名儿童的普遍建议疫苗,包括乙型肝炎。
地点与对象
The event involves the United States, specifically focusing on infants and children. The study simulated a birth cohort of 3.6 million U.S. infants through age 20, and the legal action is being pursued in federal court in Northern California.
事件
由 16 名州总检察长和宾夕法尼亚州州长组成的联盟扩大了针对 CDC 改变乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 政策决定的诉讼范围。诉讼称,这一政策改变违反了《行政程序法》,并且CDC在2025年6月更换全部17名投票委员会成员的行为是非法的。这项法律行动基于一项研究,该研究预测如果取消通用出生剂量建议,儿童乙型肝炎感染将急剧上升。
规模、影响与风险
The study projected that shared decision-making for the first hepatitis B dose could raise chronic infections in children by 83% by age 20, increasing the number of vulnerable children from about 232,000 to about 1.8 million. The policy change could lead to a significant increase in the risk of lifelong chronic hepatitis B, which raises the risk of liver cirrhosis and liver cancer decades later.
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 网站仍然建议所有婴儿接种乙型肝炎 疫苗,马萨诸塞州的一家联邦法院也保留了 2026 年 1 月 CDC 的备忘录。该研究是一个经过同行评审的计算机模型,而不是对真实感染的计数,其结果取决于对 疫苗 吸收和暴露的假设。
